This motivates a unified framework that aligns geometric and visual cues in 3D and reconstructs buildings from either or both modalities. Building on this connection, we present Any2Building, a unified model for Level of Detail 2 (LoD2) building reconstruction from partial point clouds, overhead images, or both, with optional coarse geometric priors. At its core is an occupancy-grounded shared spatial queries representation that organizes available observations within a common 3D grid. Supervised by complete building surface voxels, these queries connect observational evidence to target geometry, enabling a shared generator to learn structural priors across tasks and providing spatial guidance for recovering missing structure. A trained voxel conditional surface-generation and low-poly remeshing pipeline then converts the predicted structure into compact LoD2 building models. To support this framework, we introduce BuildingWorld-Image, comprising approximately one million paired samples of partial point clouds, overhead images, and LoD2 building models across 18 cities. Experiments on multiple benchmarks demonstrate that Any2Building outperforms existing methods in geometric reconstruction accuracy across unimodal and multimodal point-cloud completion, single-image building generation, and prior-assisted generation, while maintaining detailed and robust recovery when point cloud input modality is degraded.
  </details>

- **[VIVID: Beyond Static Multi-Image Reasoning with Localized Visual Differences and Textual Entity Dynamics in Video MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23153)**  `arXiv:2611.23153`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chengqian Ma, Yuanze Zeng, Hongyu Wang, Mingfeng Lin, YiCheng Xiao, Yu Chen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video question answering under tight frame budgets requires preserving localized motion and event order, yet current video multimodal large language models (MLLMs) reduce comprehension to static multi-image reasoning by discarding unselected frames and encoding keyframes independently: (1) Spatially, whole-frame pooling dilutes small moving targets into static backgrounds; (2) Temporally, dropping intermediate frames or averaging tokens erases inter-frame motion, confusing direction, speed, and order. We propose VIVID (Visual Inter-frame Variations and Individual Dynamics), which moves beyond static snapshots like human vision: keyframes capture core states while inter-frame Patch Diffs preserve neighboring changes within $K_f$ keyframes. Autonomous Delta-Driven Keyframe Planning (ADKP) selects anchors from a $3\text{ fps}$ pool and feeds surrounding patch differences into Dual-Road Change Memory (DRCM), forming Dynamic Tokens at zero extra anchor tokens alongside a camera-stabilized Active Motion Region of Interest (ROI) and chronological Entity State Chains. Adding $0.22\%$ trainable parameters to a frozen $27\text{B}$ backbone, VIVID-Small ($50$ examples, $\sim 1.5$ GPU hours) beats all eleven baselines in overall accuracy across $8,083$ questions and trails only VIVID-Large ($3,600$ examples), which leads on MotionBench, Video-MME, and TempCompass.
  </details>

- **[$π^4$: A Foundation Model for 4D Scene Geometry and Motion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23144)**  `arXiv:2611.23144`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifan Wang, Weiguang Zhao, Ni Yang, Haoyu Guo, Junting Dong, Weinan Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  $\pi^4$ is a unified foundation model for 4D scene perception. From diverse, uncalibrated RGB observations in the wild---a single image, an unordered collection, or a video sequence---it recovers dense geometry, camera poses, and metric scale, together with 2D/3D point tracks and moving-object masks for multi-image inputs. It couples a geometry branch with a geometry-conditioned motion branch, which predicts image-space correspondences that are lifted into 3D using reconstructed target-view geometry. Across diverse benchmarks, $\pi^4$ achieves the best overall average rank in geometry evaluation, alongside competitive performance in single- and multiview 2D/3D point tracking and moving-object segmentation. With a shared backbone, the full geometry-and-motion system contains approximately 0.5B parameters---less than half the size of most baselines. It produces joint reconstructions and tracks in one forward pass, including large-scale scenes with thousands of input images despite training with contexts of at most 64 views. We further present the data and training recipe underlying these capabilities, together with empirical studies of key design choices. Code and models are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[LUTBench: Benchmarking Text-Driven Photographic Look Transfer](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23113)**  `arXiv:2611.23113`  `cs.CV`  
  _Chuanzhi Xu, Boyu Wei, Chengkun Yue, Xuanhua Yin, Yiyang Li, Chupeng Liu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Photographic color grading shapes the tone and color of a photograph to achieve a desired look. Text-driven photographic look transfer lets users specify these adjustments in natural language, with the goal of reproducing the requested look across photographs while preserving their content. However, prior research on text-driven color grading uses different datasets, text instructions, target definitions, and metrics. These differences make it difficult to compare how faithfully the methods reproduce the same look. To compare methods under a shared protocol, we introduce LUTBench. We collect 91 three-dimensional lookup tables (3D LUTs) and define one photographic look per LUT with a specified input conversion. Applying these looks to 5,000 MIT-Adobe FiveK photographs produces 455,000 input-target image pairs with reproducible target images. For each look, we use templates to turn measured tone and color changes on fixed color samples into candidate clauses. We use training image measurements to verify applicable clauses before composing the text description. The evaluation measures target fidelity, tests sensitivity to predefined input conversions, and separates generalization to unseen looks from reproduction of seen looks on new photographs. Across 16 methods, stronger text alignment does not guarantee target fidelity. However, most supervised variants improve fidelity on both seen and unseen looks.
  </details>

- **[M3OPD-Reasoner: An Open Recipe for Post-Training Multimodal Reasoning with Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.23034)**  `arXiv:2611.23034`  `cs.CV`  
  _Nguyen Quang Trung, Hoang Phan, Shuo Sun, Longyin Zhang, AiTi Aw_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation (MOPD) enables a single student to absorb the skills of several domain teachers by learning from its own generated responses. We present Multimodal Model Multi-Teacher On-Policy Distillation Reasoner (M3OPD-Reasoner), a fully open recipe for post-training vision-language models (VLMs) over three reasoning domains: image STEM, image perception, and video. We curate two datasets, M3OPD-SFT-1.35M for supervised fine-tuning (SFT) and M3OPD-RL-193K for reinforcement learning (RL) with verifiable rewards. On these data, we compare post-training recipes that start from Qwen3.5-9B: training one model on the mixed data of all domains (MixSFT and MixRL), and training one teacher per domain with Group Sequence Policy Optimization (GSPO), directly or after an SFT warm-up, and distilling the teachers into one student with MOPD. We evaluate on 21 image STEM, image perception, and video reasoning benchmarks after we decontaminate the training data against them. The comparison establishes MOPD of teachers trained with GSPO directly from Qwen3.5-9B as the best recipe: the student closely matches each teacher on its own domain and is on average above the mixed-training baselines on the same prompts. Since MOPD already merges the domain capabilities that MixRL would otherwise have to learn, its student is a stronger starting point for RL, and we ask whether the same data can carry the model further from there: we add MixRL on top of the MOPD student, on the same prompts and with the same settings, which, to our knowledge, no prior VLM recipe has studied. The same prompts still improve the MOPD student, with no collapse, and give our main model, M3OPD-Reasoner-9B. MOPD and MixRL can therefore work as consecutive stages that get more out of the same data. We release all data, the training code, and the checkpoints of every stage.
  </details>

- **[CAST: Calibrating Correspondences for Training-Free Biomedical In-Context Segmentation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22995)**  `arXiv:2611.22995`  `cs.CV`  
  _Mingze Jiang, Jiashu Han, Haonan Chen, Zhiyi Li, Yilun Du, Kunzan Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Biomedical image segmentation must keep up with new anatomical structures, subjects, imaging modalities, and acquisition protocols, yet dense expert annotations are scarce and models trained on one cohort often struggle to transfer. In-context segmentation (ICS) offers a way out: a new structure is specified by a few annotated exemplars at inference time. However, reliable correspondence remains challenging in biomedical images, where adjacent structures often share similar appearances, and variations across subjects and imaging conditions further obscure their distinctions. We introduce CAST, a training-free framework built on a frozen DINOv3 backbone to calibrate fine-grained correspondences. Its stability test improves feature reliability at both global and patch-specific levels, while its coherence test assesses whether candidate correspondences are supported by consistent local structural context. Together, these tests enable more reliable correspondence across diverse anatomical structures and imaging conditions. Across eight biomedical benchmarks spanning 2D and 3D segmentation, cross-subject, and cross-modality transfer, CAST outperforms the strongest overall baseline by 12.34 percentage points in Dice and ranks first across all six volumetric settings. The versatility of CAST further opens up opportunities for challenging biomedical applications, including 2D-to-3D segmentation and atlas label transfer.
  </details>

- **[SpikWM: Efficient End-to-End Spiking World Modeling for Visual Planning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22981)**  `arXiv:2611.22981`  `cs.CV`  
  _Shuhan Ye, Hongbin Yu, Chong Wang, Hui Lu, Jiaming He, Yi Yu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Goal-conditioned planning with image observations and goals requires predictions that distinguish visual states and action consequences. Spiking neural networks offer sparse computation, but single-step spikes can obscure motion cues and merge actions with different physical outcomes. We propose the Spiking Latent World Model (SpikWM), an end-to-end joint-embedding predictive architecture (JEPA) with one simulation step per image and one neural update per latent position. Spiking Regional Encoding retains spatial cues through regional pooling. Bi-Spike Action Encoding combines positive and negative binary responses, while Causal Membrane Prediction carries subthreshold evidence across the latent history with learned group-wise time constants. The encoder and predictor are trained jointly on reward-free image--action trajectories using next-embedding prediction and SIGReg. With a common planner, SpikWM achieves mean success rates of $88.67\%$, $97.33\%$, $99.33\%$, and $86.67\%$ on Push-T, Two-Room, Reacher, and OGBench-Cube, respectively, outperforming two spiking baselines. Push-T ablations support each module, and controlled replays examine responses to appearance and physical state changes. Under a matched planning budget, SpikWM reduces estimated arithmetic energy by $75.6\%$ relative to LeWM. Code will be released atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SWIR-cGAN: Synthesizing SWIR Bands Using VNIR Imagery from CBERS and AMAZONIA-1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22922)**  `arXiv:2611.22922`  `cs.CV`  
  _Alisson Cleiton de Oliveira, Rennan de Freitas Bezerra Marujo, Thales Sehn Körting_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Spectral band synthesis is an important research topic in remote sensing. The Brazilian satellites CBERS-4, CBERS-4A, and AMAZONIA-1 provide high temporal resolution (1-3 days) visible and near-infrared (VNIR) imagery through the Wide-Field Imager (WFI), but lack short-wave infrared (SWIR) bands. Recent deep learning methods have shown promising results for spectral synthesis, but challenges remain for cross-satellite generalization and spectrally distant bands. This paper presents a conditional Generative Adversarial Network (cGAN) with an attention-enhanced U-net generator and a multi-loss objective to synthesize Sentinel-2/MSI SWIR bands 11 (SWIR-1) and 12 (SWIR-2) from WFI VNIR inputs. Experiments used 4,125 patches for training and cross-validation and 1,026 unseen patches for testing, with hyperparameters optimized using a subset of the training data. On SWIR-1, the model achieved SSIM of 0.900, PSNR of 31.44 dB, and MAE of 0.019 in TOA reflectance. On SWIR-2, it achieved SSIM of 0.917, PSNR of 33.35 dB, and MAE of 0.015. Benchmarking was performed on the test dataset, with each model optimized separately prior to evaluation. SWIR-cGAN outperformed the baselines, particularly for SWIR-2. The ablation study identified the multi-loss as the main performance contributor, while the attention and residual modules provided additional improvements. Our results demonstrate the feasibility of synthesizing SWIR bands from WFI imagery. The SWIR-cGAN is available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SlideJev: Order-Stable and Tissue-Grounded Jev Decisions for Whole-Slide Pathology](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22879)**  `arXiv:2611.22879`  `cs.CV`  
  _Lei Wu, Jiashuai Liu, Di Zhang, Chunze Yang, Jiusong Ge, Junbo Lu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Whole-slide visual question answering (VQA) offers a natural-language interface to pathology, allowing diagnostic and phenotypic questions to be answered directly from tissue slides. However, current vision-language models (VLMs) rely heavily on linguistic priors: their answers are sensitive to the order of the candidate answers, and can be reached through shortcuts that bypass the tissue evidence. To address these challenges, we introduce SlideJev, a framework that separately verifies candidate answers against the slide and learns to ground these judgments in tissue evidence. To connect parallel candidate evaluation with visual evidence learning, we adopt Jev and score each candidate by the difference between support and rejection log-likelihoods from the original language-model head. We compute these scores in parallel using separate prompts with the same format and no option-position labels, then normalize them for answer selection. To ground these scores in tissue evidence, candidate-conditioned reading and comparison guide gated updates to visual tokens across decoder layers. With the backbone frozen, we train these visual modules through the same scores using question-answer and paired-image supervision. Across three whole-slide pathology VQA benchmarks, SlideJev achieves the highest mean overall accuracy and 100% answer consistency across four option orders. Image-control experiments further show that SlideJev achieves the largest average accuracy gains from correct images over mismatched and image-free inputs across the three datasets. Our code is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Propose & Verify: Fast Reflection Symmetry Detection via Harmonic Fields](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22856)**  `arXiv:2611.22856`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yishai Lavi, Shai Avidan_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Detecting multiple reflection symmetries in 3D shapes requires aggregating geometric evidence over a large space of candidate planes, making traditional voting approaches computationally expensive. We introduce the Fourier Accelerated Symmetry Transform (FAST), a training-free framework that combines geometric pair voting with efficient spectral computation. Exploiting the fact that global reflection planes pass through the shape centroid, we reduce the search to plane orientations, while geometric consistency filtering and independent witnesses suppress unreliable pair hypotheses. Our key insight is to represent these votes as a signal over plane orientations and efficiently compute a continuous, smoothed symmetry field in the spherical-harmonic domain. Specifically, we exploit the Funk-Hecke theorem, the spherical counterpart of the Fourier convolution theorem for rotationally symmetric kernels, to transform Gaussian smoothing into multiplication of harmonic coefficients. This enables efficient field evaluation, from which symmetry candidates are extracted, refined, and geometrically verified. Across two benchmarks, including a new benchmark of highly symmetric shapes, FAST achieves the highest clean multi-plane detection $F_1$ scores among evaluated methods (0.861 and 0.947), with GPU inference below 4 ms per shape: over $100\times$ faster than the next-fastest evaluated multi-plane GPU baseline. FAST also demonstrates strong robustness to geometric noise and competitive single-plane detection, achieving 94.9% success on clean single-plane shapes.
  </details>

- **[Keeping Parts Honest: Isolated Part Graphs for Faithful Vision Transformer Explanations](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22853)**  `arXiv:2611.22853`  `cs.CV`  
  _Bernard Tomczyk, Gail Collyer-Hoar, Bryan Williams_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Part and prototype-based explanations for Vision Transformers (ViTs) present a decision as evidence from individual image regions. However, self-attention mixes information across patches, so part-level features absorb context from the whole image and consequently fail to faithfully reflect the decision. We show that existing interpretable ViT classifiers depend heavily on this leakage: when each explained region is encoded in isolation, their accuracy collapses, and retraining them under isolation recovers only part of the lost accuracy. We propose a classifier that represents each image as a graph of isolated parts. Each part is encoded only from its own patches by a ViT whose last blocks are fine-tuned under isolation, preventing evidence leakage from elsewhere in the image. Parts are discovered without annotation and refined hierarchically through their sub-parts, making them more consistent and stable across images than those of competing methods. Each edge is computed from part pairs, and recovers evidence that attention previously shared across the object without affecting the parts. Matching the image graph to a learned prototype graph per class makes the prediction a direct combination of node and edge similarities, each computed only from the patches it is credited to, so every explanation is faithful by construction. Across 11 fine-grained and general-object benchmarks, our method exceeds the accuracy of existing explainable methods that rely on leakage, while providing explanations that reflect what the model actually uses.
  </details>

- **[Teaching What to Look For: Privileged Perception Questions for Agentic On-Policy Distillation in Video Captioning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22755)**  `arXiv:2611.22755`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifan Xu, Ruopeng Gao, Zhixiao Wang, Jiafu Tang, Yichun Yang, Zihao Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Detailed video captioning requires comprehensive and faithful descriptions of objects, actions, and temporal events. Fixed-frame sampling, however, can miss brief motions, small objects, and other fine-grained evidence, motivating captioning agents that actively seek relevant moments and inspect local regions. Training such behavior is challenging because, unlike video question answering, generic captioning instructions do not specify what visual evidence should be acquired. We propose Perception-Question On-Policy Distillation (PQ-OPD), which uses training-only Perception Questions (PQs) to guide active video perception. PQs specify what should be inspected without revealing the corresponding answers, encouraging the teacher to explore the video more comprehensively and focus on relevant visual evidence. Along trajectories generated by the student, on-policy distillation transfers this guidance to the student's reasoning, tool calls, and captions; no PQs are required at inference. Across three detailed-captioning and two caption-to-QA benchmarks, PQ-OPD consistently improves the corresponding untrained agents across teacher-student scales and input-frame budgets. Under our evaluation protocol, PQ-OPD achieves the strongest primary-metric results among the compared open-source models on DREAM-1K, CaReBench, ShortVidBench, MotionBench, and VidCapBench-AE. Our ablations, together with analysis of temporal coverage and answer leakage, further indicates that the student internalizes the training-time guidance as a more effective video exploration policy.
  </details>

- **[GaussianMamba: Geometric and Relational State Space Scanning of 3D Gaussian Primitives](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22742)**  `arXiv:2611.22742`  `cs.CV`  
  _A. Enes Doruk, Ozgur Erkent_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  3D semantic occupancy prediction requires representing complex spatial structures and modeling contextual relationships across a scene. 3D Gaussian primitives provide a sparse and adaptive representation of scene geometry and semantics, while Mamba offers efficient sequence modeling for capturing dependencies among primitives. However, Gaussian primitives are irregular and unordered, making sequence construction challenging, as conventional spatial ordering may overlook their anisotropic geometry and feature-semantic relationships. In this paper, we present GaussianMamba, a dual-branch Mamba framework that explicitly incorporates Gaussian-specific geometric and feature-semantic relationships into complementary scanning strategies. The first branch refines a locality-preserving traversal using anisotropic Gaussian support derived from primitive scale and orientation. The second branch reorders spatially organized groups according to feature-semantic affinity and adjusts local features using geometry-weighted semantic differences. The resulting sequences are processed by separate Mamba modules, and their features are fused through a support-aware mechanism that incorporates local feature discrepancies arising from the distinct scanning paths. Experiments on nuScenes-SurroundOcc and SSCBench-KITTI-360 demonstrate consistent improvements over GaussianFormer and GaussianFormer-2 in both IoU and mIoU.
  </details>

- **[Evolve in Panorama, Render in View: Panoramic World State Evolution for Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22733)**  `arXiv:2611.22733`  `cs.CV`  
  _Longtao Jiang, Mingfei Han, Jianmin Bao, Tingjun Dai, Jinjie Wei, Zhiran Zhang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Video world models should maintain a world that keeps evolving as the viewpoint changes. Yet when the camera turns away and returns, current models often freeze the unobserved scene, lose objects, or change their appearance, because content outside the view has no representation that keeps changing. We present PanoEvo, which uses a Panoramic Evolver to evolve the world state in the latent space of a panoramic video diffusion model, where every viewing direction is present at every time step, so the scene evolves whether or not it is observed. Existing panoramic video models are not trained to evolve a scene from a single image or the objects within it, so we build PanoEvo-Data, an event-centric dataset from real 360° videos, and train the Panoramic Evolver coarse-to-fine as an image-to-panoramic-video model. Since each view covers only a small part of the low-resolution panorama, directly projected views are blurry and often show low-quality details or structural errors. A Perspective Renderer, built on a perspective video diffusion model, reads this state via latent-space projection and direction-restricted cross-attention to render sharp, high-quality views. PanoEvo achieves the best overall score among 23 publicly evaluated models on WRBench and the highest total score on STEVO-Bench. Code, models, and data will be released.
  </details>

- **[SlopeGuard: Towards Generalizable AI-Generated Image Detection via Radial Spectral Slope Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22727)**  `arXiv:2611.22727`  `cs.CV`  
  _Cheng Fu, Yiheng Li, Yang Yang, Wenzheng Liu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  AI-generated image detectors often overfit to generator-specific artifacts, hindering generalization to unseen synthesis pipelines. Existing frequency-based methods usually learn high-dimensional spectral patterns that may preserve generator-dependent variations. We explore Radial Spectral Slope, a compact descriptor of global spectral decay that captures transferable spectral trends while suppressing fine-grained variations. Our analysis shows that real and generated images exhibit systematic but generator-dependent slope differences, indicating that slope provides useful forensic evidence but is insufficient as a standalone decision criterion. This motivates combining spectral slope with semantic representations for more robust detection. Based on this insight, we propose SlopeGuard, which couples slope-aware data processing with slope guided feature learning to construct informative patch-based views, integrate Radial Spectral Slope with semantic features, and adapt prior preservation according to spectral atypicality. Extensive experiments demonstrate that SlopeGuard achieves state-of-the-art mean accuracies of 97.5% on AIGCDetectBench, 97.8% on GenImage, and 85.1% on AIGIBench, while maintaining competitive performance under blur, cropping, JPEG compression, and additive noise.
  </details>

- **[PCRG: Training-Free Pruning and Caching of Reference Tokens for Video Generation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22717)**  `arXiv:2611.22717`  `cs.CV`  
  _Siyuan Wang, Haoran Li, Yi Yue, Tak L. Fung, Quan Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reference-to-video diffusion transformers pack the variational autoencoder (VAE) latents of all references and the generated tokens into one full-attention sequence, in which reference tokens make up about half. Because attention cost grows quadratically with sequence length, these tokens take up an even larger share of the attention computation, yet most of them are static, smooth or irrelevant to the output. To accelerate reference-conditioned generation, we propose pruning and caching of reference tokens for video generation (PCRG), a training-free, plug-and-play method that uses two signals the model already computes to assign each reference token to one of three tiers, namely pruned, cached or kept. The first is a low-level redundancy r from VAE latents, since a token whose latent barely changes over time or across its neighbors adds little new content. The second is a high-level importance g from the vision-language-model (VLM) conditioner, where the attention that the prompt and any first or last frames pay to a token tells whether the output needs to read it. Tokens that are not redundant are kept, and redundant tokens are cached if important and pruned otherwise. Pruned tokens leave the sequence before the first step with all rotary position embedding positions unchanged. Cached tokens have their keys and values computed once and reused, and kept tokens are processed as usual. On MiniMax-H3, already distilled to 8 timesteps and quantized to int8, PCRG is 1.57x faster end to end on our V2V-Bench and 1.49x faster on the public VACE-Benchmark, while the quality of VBench drops by only 0.24% and 0.17%, respectively. PCRG shortens the sequence rather than changing how attention is computed or which steps are run, so it also stacks on step caching and sparse attention. On V2V-Bench it reaches 1.86x with TeaCache, 1.82x with Spark at nearly unchanged fidelity and 2.15x with Veda.
  </details>

- **[TANGO: Real-World Unregistered RGB-Guided Hyperspectral Super-Resolution with Two-Camera Acquisitions](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22699)**  `arXiv:2611.22699`  `cs.CV`  
  _Matteo Kolyszko, Marco Buzzelli, Simone Bianco, Raimondo Schettini_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Unregistered hyperspectral image super-resolution aims to recover a high-resolution HSI from a low-resolution hyperspectral observation and an unregistered high-resolution RGB reference. Although recent methods have begun to address the geometric misalignment between the two modalities, their evaluation is still constrained by the lack of a large-scale public benchmark based on real two-camera RGB--HSI acquisitions with a high-resolution HSI reference. To address this gap, we introduce DUET, the first large-scale public dataset for unregistered RGB-guided hyperspectral super-resolution, comprising 403 indoor and outdoor scenes captured with two separate RGB and hyperspectral cameras. We further propose TANGO (Two-camera AligNment with Gated Occlusions), which first compensates for the dominant cross-camera displacement through end-to-end global alignment and then refines the remaining inconsistencies with a coarse-to-fine local feature alignment. A learned gating mechanism further attenuates the RGB guidance where the two views cannot be consistently matched. Experimental results show that TANGO achieves state-of-the-art performance on both DUET and REAL across all evaluated scale factors, outperforming the strongest competitor on DUET by up to 2.29 dB in PSNR while requiring 2.5$\times$ fewer FLOPs. The code and dataset will be available atthis https URL
  </details>

- **[Let Samples Sing in Chorus: Mitigating Spurious Correlations via Cross-view Consensus](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22668)**  `arXiv:2611.22668`  `cs.CV`  
  _Weiwei Li, Junzhuo Liu, Tong Chu, Hengfu Yu, Yinjie Lei, Wen Li_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deep models often rely on spurious correlations that fail on underrepresented groups. Existing methods either correct human-specified bias attributes or infer unknown biases from the learned representation space. However, annotated attributes may differ from the shortcuts actually used by a model, while a single representation provides incomplete and potentially noisy evidence. Our key insight is that a bias obscured in one feature space may be clearer in another. In this work, we propose CHORD (Cross-view Harmonic Outlier Reversal for Debiasing), a multi-view framework that allows samples to "sing in chorus" across diverse representation spaces to achieve robust cross-view consensus. CHORD aggregates aggregates within-class partitions in each view, which is a proxy for specifying candidate biases, to debias the target representation and classifier without bias supervision. Experiments on seven benchmarks across vision, language, speech, and medical imaging show consistent improvements in worst-group accuracy. Code and checkpoints are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SpatialCast: Reading the 3D World via Cast Shadows](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22657)**  `arXiv:2611.22657`  `cs.CV`  
  _Enhan Li, Jinghao Zhou, Jiaze Zhang, Hongyang Du_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  As multimodal large language models (MLLMs) advance toward understanding and interacting with the physical world, spatial reasoning becomes essential for understanding scene structure and anticipating the effects of physical changes. Existing spatial benchmarks have made substantial progress in evaluating spatial relations, memory, and viewpoint reasoning, but less systematically assess how models connect physical scene conditions with visual observations. We introduce SpatialCast, a benchmark that uses cast shadows to evaluate this ability. Cast shadows provide observable evidence of a 3D scene. Their image appearance depends on object geometry and pose, illumination, and viewpoint. We examine this relationship through three complementary tasks that assess scene-projection consistency, predict intervention-induced changes, and infer the physical changes behind visual transitions. The benchmark contains 21,000 synthetic questions with separate scene and geometry test partitions, complemented by 200 SpatialCast-Real questions from 20 photographed scenes. Evaluations show that closed-source MLLMs outperform general-purpose open-weight MLLMs and MLLMs designed for spatial reasoning, which remain near chance on the synthetic benchmark, while even strong closed-source models struggle with shadow-length and light-height judgments. We also use SpatialCast training data for GRPO post-training, improving open-weight MLLM performance on the benchmark. Further evaluations show improvements on several external spatial benchmarks, suggesting transfer of the learned capabilities. These experiments support SpatialCast as a controlled resource for diagnosing spatial reasoning capabilities and studying their learning and generalization.
  </details>

- **[OmniTongue: A Comprehensive MLLM Benchmark for Automated Tongue Diagnosis in Traditional Chinese Medicine](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22652)**  `arXiv:2611.22652`  `cs.CV`  
  _Liuyi Xu, Yun Guo, Shusheng Li, Xuewen Zhang, Yining Qian, An-Yang Lu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal Large Language Models (MLLMs) have excelled in Western medical imaging, yet their potential in Traditional Chinese Medicine (TCM) tongue diagnosis remains systematically unevaluated. Tongue diagnosis requires sophisticated global and local perception, serving as a rigorous touchstone for assessing the visual understanding depth of MLLMs. However, existing datasets are limited by single dimensions, coarse granularity, and ambiguous labels, hindering the evaluation of authentic clinical capabilities. To bridge this gap, we introduce OmniTongue, the first comprehensive, fine-grained Visual Question Answering benchmark for TCM tongue diagnosis. This benchmark integrates 1,351 expert-validated images and 21,616 instruction pairs. Its core design features 16 clinical dimensions for multidimensional evaluation, region-specific annotations for pathological localization, and 66 fine-grained labels for precise diagnostic representation. Extensive evaluations of existing MLLMs reveal significant performance gaps, demonstrating that medical-specific models lack domain advantages, while universally poor Macro-F1 scores expose a reliance on statistical shortcuts rather than genuine understanding of tongue pathology. These findings not only highlight the current limitations of MLLMs in interpreting regional medical features but also underscore OmniTongue's significance for developing robust automated tongue diagnosis models. The code and dataset will be made publicly available in a future release.
  </details>

- **[Must You See Fakes to Catch Fakes? Language Says What to Look For, Real Images What to Ignore](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22650)**  `arXiv:2611.22650`  `cs.CV`  
  _Tianchi Xu, Yuxuan Zhou, Yuheng Zhang, Yanan Guan, Tao Dai, Shu-Tao Xia_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Must a detector see generated images in order to catch them? Detection of AI-generated images has long faced two problems. The cues a detector learns from the few generators in its training set carry traces specific to those generators and transfer poorly to new ones, which limits generalization. The real and generated training images also differ in format and content, so the detector takes these differences as evidence of authenticity, and its decisions change with factors that have nothing to do with authenticity, which harms robustness as well as generalization. Both problems stem from training on generated images. A detector never trained on them can largely avoid both at the source. Vision foundation models have already learned the concept of "AI-generated" during pretraining, and text can point to it directly. Building on this, we propose SaySee, a training-free detector: Say builds a generation direction from sets of paired descriptions that differ only in authenticity and scores an image by the projection of its embedding onto it, and See uses real images to correct the score offsets caused by format, image type and content. On 24 evaluation sets that span standard benchmarks, in-the-wild data and the latest generators, SaySee reaches a mean AUC of 98.2% with MoE-ViE, 4.2 points above the best existing detector. When format is decoupled from authenticity, it remains the best of all methods. Code will be released.
  </details>

- **[REVEAL: Reconstructing Video Amodal Content via Language and Flow Guidance](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22645)**  `arXiv:2611.22645`  `cs.CV`  
  _Minh Tran, Winston Bounsavy, Ngan Le_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Amodal perception enables humans to perceive entire objects even when parts are occluded, a remarkable cognitive skill that artificial intelligence struggles to replicate. Recent diffusion-based methods have extended amodal completion from images to videos, yet they lack auxiliary priors to guide the reconstruction of heavily occluded content and to maintain temporal consistency over long sequences. We present REVEAL (REconstructing VidEo Amodal content via Language and flow guidance), a unified diffusion-based framework that integrates complementary motion and semantic priors for video amodal completion. A user-provided text query serves a dual role: it drives open-vocabulary video segmentation to obtain visible masks, and it provides semantic guidance for texture reconstruction. For amodal segmentation, we introduce optical flow guidance: by warping visible masks from previous frames to the current frame, the flow-warped mask propagates visible content into occluded regions and approximates the object's current shape, providing a strong shape prior even under simultaneous deformation and occlusion. For texture completion, text guidance constrains the reconstruction of occluded appearance while providing a stable semantic reference that helps maintain visual coherence throughout the reconstruction. We also contribute LAVAT, a new benchmark featuring long video sequences paired with text descriptions, enabling evaluation of text-guided video amodal completion under heavy occlusion. Extensive experiments demonstrate that REVEAL achieves state-of-the-art performance on existing benchmarks while maintaining high-quality temporal consistency over extended sequences. The project page is available at:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[PlaceTally: A Test-Time Memory Model for Constructing Place Graphs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22608)**  `arXiv:2611.22608`  `cs.CV`  
  _Alibay Osmanli, Shaogang Gong_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Counting distinct objects in a video is hard when the camera returns to places it has seen. A vision-language model that reads the whole video answers with a few small numbers, and one that reads it in parts counts an object again in every part that shows it. Object trackers need the category before the video is read, or a fixed vocabulary. We observe that a memory for counting does not need to know what it is looking for, only where it has been: objects rarely leave a room between visits, so parts of a video grouped by place share almost no objects, and a place can be recognised before any question is asked. Building on this, we introduce PlaceTally, a question-blind test-time memory model that constructs a place graph for each video. Each video fragment joins the place of its best earlier match or opens a new place, because a strong match is rarely wrong while a true return often matches weakly. A frozen vision-language model counts each place once from at most 128 of its frames, so every read has a bounded input and the memory grows with the number of places; the graph is built 15 times faster than real time. On RevisitCount, our benchmark of real room re-recordings, it scores 62.5% mean relative accuracy on test rooms and 61.2% on held-out rooms, against 51.2% and 44.3% for the same model reading the whole video. It stays level as rooms return more or less often, while a fixed correction of summed visits moves from 38.5% to 61.4%.
  </details>

- **[Semantic-Guided Geometric Decoding for Token-Efficient Visual Grounding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22577)**  `arXiv:2611.22577`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhaojie Chen, Junyan Yang, Le Yang, Qi Dang, Yucheng Huang, Qianyi Che, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) provide a unified interface for language-conditioned visual perception. In pixel-to-sequence grounding, autoregressive coordinate generation requires multiple sequential decoding steps per box, increasing inference cost with the number of instances. Pixel-to-embedding methods reduce this overhead but lack explicit spatial encoding when using a shared special token. We introduce SemGeo, a token-efficient visual grounding framework that represents each instance with a single grid token for coarse localization. A geometric decoder reuses visual features and hidden states to recover precise discrete box coordinates in parallel. To train proposal generation and box localization together, we introduce Gaussian soft labels that encode coordinate distances during supervised fine-tuning (SFT). Reinforcement post-training further combines box-set rewards with coordinate supervision on model-generated proposals. This stage trains the language model and geometric decoder on their own predictions to mitigate the mismatch between teacher-forced training and inference. Across eight benchmarks spanning object detection, referring object detection, and document layout grounding, SemGeo achieves the highest F1@mIou among compared MLLMs on six benchmarks. It also delivers $2.07\times$ and $1.25\times$ the mean box-generation throughput of representative grounding baselines under vLLM and Transformers, respectively, while achieving higher accuracy in both comparisons.
  </details>

- **[Disagree to Prune: Reallocating Visual Tokens Where a Small VLM Dissents](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22472)**  `arXiv:2611.22472`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yanchao Li, Jiaqing Xie, Ben Gao, Yusu Zhang, Beilun Wang, Yanbo Wang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual token pruning speeds up large vision-language models (VLMs) by keeping only a part of their visual tokens. Most existing methods improve the ranking of the tokens and then answer every question once with the same budget. With strong rankings, pruning hurts only a few questions, and signals computed on the kept tokens see little of what it removed. We find that, on most of these questions, a small VLM given the full image disagrees with the pruned answer, since it still sees the missing evidence. We propose PRUDENT, which first answers with about half the budget and spends the saved tokens on re-answering the disagreeing questions at twice the budget. We show when such re-answering pays and why the small VLM should read the full image. On 18 benchmarks, PRUDENT outperforms eight existing pruning methods at the same average budget of visual tokens. Its lead widens as the budget tightens, and holds across five InternVL sizes. PRUDENT also has the highest average in two more model families. The disagreement of the small VLM flags most pruning failures of all eight methods, and re-answering on it also improves the two strongest with their own rankings. Other checkers give similar gains, and the extra tokens go where the first pass hurts the answer. These results suggest that identifying which pruned answers are trustworthy provides an additional lever for visual token pruning beyond the ranking of tokens. Code and data are available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[Can Vision-Language Models Truly Understand Images? A Benchmark Beyond Commonsense Bias](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22360)**  `arXiv:2611.22360`  `cs.CV`  
  _Ling Lin, Heng Su, Xiangzeng Liu, Yang Bai, Congcong Zhu, Jingrun Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal large language models (MLLMs) have achieved impressive performance on a wide range of vision-language tasks. However, it remains unclear whether their predictions are grounded in the visual content itself or are largely driven by statistical co-occurrence patterns and commonsense correlations learned from large-scale image-text corpora. Existing vision-language benchmarks predominantly consist of samples that conform to human commonsense, allowing models to achieve strong performance by exploiting dataset priors rather than genuinely understanding image content. To address this limitation, we present ContraSense, a systematically constructed benchmark for evaluating faithful vision-language understanding. ContraSense consists of carefully curated counter-intuitive image-text pairs that deliberately violate common expectations, substantially reducing the effectiveness of statistical biases and requiring models to rely on visual evidence for reasoning. We conduct comprehensive evaluations on a diverse set of representative open- and closed-source MLLMs. The results reveal a substantial performance gap between current MLLMs and humans: even the strongest closed-source model achieves only about 60\% accuracy, compared with 96.04\% for human participants. These findings demonstrate that current MLLMs still struggle with faithful visual understanding and highlight the need for more robust multimodal reasoning grounded in image content. The ContraSense benchmark is publicly available atthis https URL.
  </details>

- **[SandBAR: A Band-Asymmetric Architecture for Underwater Image Restoration with Removable Frequency Scaffolding](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22355)**  `arXiv:2611.22355`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yanzi Liu, Haoran Hou, Jiachi Tong, Shen Chen, Shuang Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Light scattering, absorption and turbidity degrade underwater imagery through complementary frequency tendencies: large-scale, channel-dependent appearance distortions and local high-frequency detail loss. This band asymmetry motivates different forms of restoration for global colour and local structure. Our primary contribution is SandBAR, a novel unified band-asymmetric architecture for underwater image restoration. Rather than treating frequency processing as an isolated module, SandBAR organises the restoration pipeline around band asymmetry at the block, encoder and objective levels. Within this architecture, we introduce a novel band-asymmetric sandwich block with a reserved post-attention repair slot and the Split-band Parallel Asymmetric Repair Kernel (SPARK), a novel module that applies channel-aware large-support correction to the low band, which predominantly carries large-scale appearance distortions, and lightweight local refinement to the detail-bearing high band, thereby restoring global colour while preserving fine structure. To deliver the global cast to the depth at which the network represents global structure, a learnable low-frequency guidance path injects a low-pass view of the input into the deep encoder, and a three-scale spatial-frequency objective supervises the same axis throughout the decoder. Building on the reserved post-attention slot, we further introduce Post-attention Scaffold Training (PaST), a novel removable nonlinear scaffolding scheme that trains the permanent SandBAR graph under band-specific capacity and runs it at inference without that capacity. SandBAR reaches 29.89 / 28.92 / 22.77 dB on EUVP / LSUI / UIEB and establishes a new three-domain mean state of the art on the benchmark family, exceeding the strongest published result by approximately 1.0 dB. PaST adds no parameters or FLOPs at inference and preserves matched latency.
  </details>

- **[PURE: A Diagnostic Benchmark for Multi-Granularity Multimodal Identity Matching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22343)**  `arXiv:2611.22343`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hengyu You, Xintong Wang, Jin Cao, Liang Ding_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Identity matching asks whether two records denote the same identity, yet the answer depends on the target granularity. Two records may match at the base level but not at the variant level: they share an underlying design while differing in attributes such as color or size. Final matching accuracy does not separately characterize the capabilities involved. To study this problem, we use e-commerce as a challenging real-world domain. We introduce PURE, a diagnostic benchmark for multi-granularity multimodal identity matching covering four capability dimensions: Perception, Understanding, Reasoning, and Evidence-grounded Judgment. The benchmark comprises 1,457 multiple-choice questions across 14 tasks, derived from real-world matching pairs and verified by two domain experts. Across ten recent MLLMs, overall scores range from 47.1% to 86.0%. Understanding receives the highest mean score and Judgment the lowest, with weaknesses in visual difference localization and variant-level rule judgment. Modality ablations show that models benefit from multimodal evidence but can incorrectly identify conflicts between matching records. In direct matching, most models recognize matching pairs more reliably than non-matching pairs at the base level, whereas the reverse holds at the variant level. PURE therefore reveals what aggregate matching accuracy hides: not only whether models judge a pair correctly, but where they struggle to interpret evidence and apply matching criteria.
  </details>

- **[SARVIA: Fast-Adapting the Strong SAR Foundation Models That Are Already in Your MLLMs](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22273)**  `arXiv:2611.22273`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yuxuan Li, Zichen Tian, Qianru Sun_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Synthetic aperture radar (SAR) foundation models provide transferable representations for diverse perception tasks in all-weather Earth observation. However, their development often relies on costly large-scale SAR pretraining, raising the question of whether such extensive domain-specific pretraining is necessary. We find that modern multimodal large language models (MLLMs) already provide visual encoders that can outperform dedicated SAR foundation models as downstream initializations. This motivates a new paradigm: reuse general visual capabilities and devote domain training to brief specialization. We therefore introduce SARVIA, an adaptive post-pretraining method that specializes modern MLLMs using self-supervised visual questions with computable answers. These questions are constructed from unlabeled observations through general image tasks that capture spatial relations and SAR-specific tasks that exploit native measurements and SAR--optical correspondence. A shared answer-prediction objective jointly trains low-rank adapters across visual and language components, with loss-driven task sampling. We then export the adapted visual encoder as a standalone backbone. With 600 adaptation steps and 2 GPU-hours, SARVIA outperforms the compared foundation models on all six SAR detection and segmentation benchmarks. Compared with dedicated SAR pretraining, SARVIA reduces domain-training costs by orders of magnitude while improving visual transfer across architectures and scales. This new paradigm shifts the emphasis from repeated, costly pretraining to a reusable specialization process that can benefit from advances in open MLLMs. Dataset and code:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[PhyVGGT: Feed-Forward Articulated Object Reconstruction from Monocular Videos](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22244)**  `arXiv:2611.22244`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qi Shao, Zhihao Cao, Shuhao Zhai, Anh Nguyen, Hong Liang, Baoru Huang, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Reconstructing an articulated object with its 3D movable parts, joint parameters and unseen articulation states from an uncalibrated monocular video is essential for robot manipulation and interactive digital twins. Existing per-scene optimisation methods require minutes to hours and external depth or camera poses, whereas feed-forward 3D foundation models reconstruct dense point maps in one pass without segmenting movable parts or inferring joints. Even on these monocular point maps, regressing or fitting continuous joints directly fails on casual videos, since cross-frame attention blurs moving part boundaries in deep layers, underestimates motion along the viewing ray and leaves continuous axes ill-conditioned under small opening angles. To address these challenges, we propose PhyVGGT, a single-pass framework on a frozen 3D foundation model that anchors moving-part matching and closed-form joint estimation to the undistorted geometry of the stationary base. First, our Part and Motion Decoder fuses intermediate per-frame tokens with global tokens and references part queries to a static-base query to segment 3D parts, align point maps on the static base and predict learned Bingham priors. Second, our Kinematic Inference Layer fits an orthogonal Manhattan frame to static surface normals, selects the joint axis in closed form under a Bingham posterior combining pairwise motion scatter with the learned priors, and solves a linear system for the hinge pivot to drive unseen states. Trained solely on synthetic data, PhyVGGT achieves state-of-the-art monocular performance across five benchmarks, outperforming FAMOS and monocular ArtiPoint, surpassing posed RGB-D ArtiPoint in median error, and running in 6.8 s per 48-frame video against hours of per-scene optimisation in Articulat3D. Project page:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[CoSphere-SLAM: Multi-Agent Panoramic Gaussian Splatting SLAM with Spherical Loop Closure](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22235)**  `arXiv:2611.22235`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qi Shao, Zhihao Cao, Shuhao Zhai, Taoyu Wu, Zhuoxiao Li, Anh Nguyen, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Dense collaborative mapping is essential for search and rescue, building inspection, and warehouse automation, where panoramic cameras capture the entire surroundings in a single frame. Unlike pinhole agents that share views only at similar headings, panoramic agents observe overlapping scenes at any 3D orientation, yet searching full rotations also produces mutually consistent false loop closures across distinct symmetric rooms. Moreover, continuous monocular scale drift splits rigidly merged maps at revisited surfaces, while depth noise at short baselines distorts loop translation directions. To address these challenges, we propose CoSphere-SLAM, the first multi-agent Gaussian splatting SLAM framework for panoramic cameras, whose spherical loop closure aligns spherical features over $\mathrm{SO}(3)$ via fast Fourier correlation across arbitrary orientations and rejects symmetric false closures through global $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ consensus. Building on these closures, directional global optimization solves a per-keyframe $\mathrm{Sim}(3)$ pose graph with log-scale regularization to correct scale drift while separating optical-flow translation directions from depth-based magnitudes. Finally, omnidirectional Gaussian fusion transforms each Gaussian with its keyframe similarity and renders Gaussians at their maximum ray response to jointly refine poses and maps. Experiments on indoor and cross-session multi-agent panoramic benchmarks demonstrate that CoSphere-SLAM achieves state-of-the-art trajectory accuracy and rendering quality among single-agent and multi-agent methods. Project website:this https URL.
  </details>

- **[ExplicitIR: Explicit Spatial Policy Fields for All-in-One Image Restoration](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22222)**  `arXiv:2611.22222`  `cs.CV`  
  _Dezhi Zhang, Shangrong Yang, Yudong Liang, Wenjian Wang_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  All-in-one image restoration aims to recover clean images blindly from inputs with various degradation types and levels using a single model. However, the adaptation policy in most existing methods remains implicitly encoded in feature representations, leaving the restoration decision neither explicitly represented nor independently controllable. Since real-world degradations vary spatially, applying uniform updates across the entire image leads to over-smoothing of clean areas and under-correction of localized degradations. To address these limitations, we present ExplicitIR, which represents the restoration policy as two explicit per-pixel fields: Budget, which determines where and how strongly to update, and Style, which selects between narrow and wide context windows. Specifically, ExplicitIR incorporates a Spatial Policy Estimator (SPE) to predict these fields, explicitly representing the restoration policy and capturing spatially-varying degradations, which are then applied by Policy-Adaptive Decoder Blocks (PADB) through adaptive fusion of narrow- and wide-window responses, enabling independent control and precise local modulation. This design enables spatially-adaptive restoration in a single forward pass, without requiring task labels, prompts, or test-time degradation estimators. Extensive experiments on three-task and five-task benchmarks demonstrate that ExplicitIR achieves performance competitive with the strongest all-in-one baselines.
  </details>

- **[HandRSI: Recursive Self-Improvement for Hand Pose Estimation](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22164)**  `arXiv:2611.22164`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhekai Wang, Luqing Luo, Wenhao Yao, Junjie Lu, Huanan Zhu, Feifei Gao, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Egocentric 3D hand pose estimators still miss hands at the image border, misplace occluded hands and jitter. Such failures are usually fixed by hand-written inference refinements and by fine-tuning, two interdependent steps that are tuned separately. We present HandRSI, a recursive self-improvement framework for a hand pose system that consists of a video estimator and its executable inference harness. In each round, coding agents refine the harness, and a change is accepted only if it improves a composite of all ten benchmark metrics on held-out data without significantly regressing any of them. The accepted harness then guides the estimator through selective distillation, which supervises hand presence and temporal consistency on frames without ground truth, while ground truth supervises pose. Since the updated estimator may change which refinements remain useful, the harness is re-validated on it. Starting from the released ACE-Ego-Hand model, five rounds reduce MPJPE-p by 35% on HOT3D and 11% on ARCTIC and roughly halve jitter. On HOT3D, HandRSI's MPJPE-p is 16% lower than that of ACE-Ego-Hand fine-tuned on the same ground truth. Under a unified protocol, it outperforms nine re-run published methods on all ten metrics of both benchmarks. Hypervolume selection on the accuracy-speed front yields Lite, Medium and Heavy systems running at 20.7 to 41.1 FPS. This report gives the full method, every stage of every round and the complete protocol. Code, evaluator, harnesses and weights are public.
  </details>

- **[BATMoS: Budget-Aware Guidance Allocation for Efficient Video Diffusion](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.22028)**  `arXiv:2611.22028`  `cs.CV`  
  _Zhixu Du, Kuo Yang, Jingwei Sun, Hai "Helen" Li, Yiran Chen_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  The effectiveness of a video diffusion model's inference budget depends on where computation is spent along the denoising trajectory. Across eight equal-budget inference configurations, VBench total scores span 4.80 points, highlighting the importance of explicit allocation design. Our key insight is that guidance evaluations are not equally valuable across diffusion time: retaining the sampling updates while selectively reusing guidance can preserve generation quality with fewer denoiser evaluations. We introduce BATMoS (Budget Aware Temporal Mixture of Strategies), which routes between fresh classifier-free guidance and cached guidance using a single pretrained denoiser. A magnitude-based temporal rule assigns guidance reuse to low-magnitude blocks and reserves fresh guidance for the remaining blocks, with a single integer controlling the computation budget. The resulting schedule requires no additional router training or per-budget threshold search. Evaluated on CogVideoX-2B with 4,720 videos per configuration across all sixteen official VBench dimensions, BATMoS achieves the highest total score among the eight allocation policies and comes within 0.10 points of full classifier-free guidance using 20\% fewer branch evaluations. BATMoS also composes with feature reuse, reducing computation from 80 to 62 effective branch evaluations with a 0.11-point decrease in total score. These findings make temporal guidance allocation a practical mechanism for extracting more generation quality from a fixed inference budget.
  </details>

- **[Beyond Appearance: Where Forest Visual Place Recognition Actually Fails](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21996)**  `arXiv:2611.21996`  `cs.CV`  
  _Blake Matheson, Ben McShanag, Isabella Moffat, Michael Milford, Peyman Moghadam, Dana Kulić, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Visual place recognition (VPR) in forest environments remains challenging because repetitive vegetation, and changes in illumination, season, and viewpoint can make distinct places appear similar and revisited places appear substantially different. In this paper, we investigate whether temporal, linguistic, structural, and geometric context can improve on an appearance-only descriptor, and what limits each representation when it does not. Using MegaLoc as the appearance-based baseline on the WildCross benchmark, we evaluate score-space and descriptor-space sequence matching, vision-language captions and reranking, scene-graph representations, and depth-derived stem graphs across same-direction revisits, extended routes, and reverse traversals. On a same-direction revisit, both sequence matching strategies reach similar Recall@1 (94.1% vs. 91.5%), but recall at 100% precision rises from 50.9% (single-frame MegaLoc) to 71.6% in score space and falls to 36.5% in descriptor space. The remaining context representations provide no consistent improvement over the appearance-based baseline. Same-place captions show limited place-specific discrimination, reranking is constrained by the initial candidate shortlist, and stem-graph matching remains weak even with LiDAR depth.
  </details>

- **[GridDriver: On-Board BEV-Based Language Planning and Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21924)**  `arXiv:2611.21924`  `cs.CV`  
  _Katharina Winter, William Engel, Zain A. Alishammat, Fabian B. Flohr_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Bird's-Eye-View (BEV)-conditioned large language models (LLMs) enable language-interactive driving, but onboard deployment requires efficient perception and continuous planning alongside autoregressive language generation. We present GridDriver, an efficient BEV-conditioned LLM framework combining trajectory prediction, structured driving indicators, natural-language navigation, driving explanations, and scene question answering. Lightweight spatio-temporal BEV perception and spatially structured tokens provide scene context to a shared LLM, while frame-wise scheduling interleaves trajectory prediction with asynchronous language generation. GridDriver achieves competitive performance on public benchmarks, reaching 87.9% PDMS on NAVSIM and 57.6% accuracy on nuScenes-QA with a shared planning and QA checkpoint. Spatial representation analyses demonstrate improved geometric information retention and trajectory prediction compared with Q-Former compression. The system achieves 32.7 ms standalone planning latency and 9.4 plans/s in an onboard ROS 2 pipeline. We further evaluate planning on our own data recordings and demonstrate open-loop onboard operation on a research vehicle. Across public benchmarks and real-world evaluations, we identify strengths and limitations of the BEV-based approach.
  </details>

- **[Can a World Model Predict a Deepfake Video? A Consistency Margin Prior for Deepfake Detection](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21846)**  `arXiv:2611.21846`  `cs.CV`  
  _Pradip Gupta, Shubham Mandloi, Ravi Kumar Satzoda, Sujeeth Bharadwaj, Yassine Benajiba, Dan Roth_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Deepfake detectors often perform well on the dataset they are trained on, but their performance drops when tested on videos from different datasets. In this paper, we study whether a predictive video model can provide a signal that generalizes better across datasets. Instead of relying only on generator-specific artifacts, as many existing detectors do, we use a model trained to predict natural video and measure the difference between what it predicts and what it observes in a video. Using V-JEPA 2.1, we keep the encoder frozen and adapt the predictor using rank-16 LoRA. We define the difference between the predictor output and the frozen encoder target as the prediction residual. We hypothesize that manipulated clips produce, on average, larger prediction residuals than authentic clips, and use this as our consistency-margin prior. We process these residuals using a lightweight Latent Consistency Head (LCH), which separates residual magnitude from direction for training and classification. To test whether the direction of the consistency margin affects cross-dataset generalization, we compare standard and reversed margin objectives while keeping the remaining training setup fixed. We match the two settings on FF++ validation performance before evaluating them on unseen data. The reversed-margin setting exhibits a larger cross dataset generalization gap on average, providing evidence that the direction of the consistency margin affects cross-dataset transfer. Our complete model adds only 8.25M trainable parameters, which is 2.47% of the total 334M parameters. Trained only on FF++, it achieves an AUC of 90.5 on Celeb-DFv2 and performs well across several other cross-dataset benchmarks. Our ablations show that separating residual magnitude from direction, reducing dataset-specific variation, and using a low-rank projection improve cross-dataset generalization in our experiments.
  </details>

- **[Rethinking Perceptual Similarity for Remote Sensing: Multi-Layer Attention Scoring and Human Preference Benchmarking](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21816)**  `arXiv:2611.21816`  `cs.CV`  
  _Eli Underwood, Marc Bosch, Neil Joshi, Jeffrey Wen, Myron Brown_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Perceptual similarity metrics for natural images transfer poorly to remote sensing (RS), where severe geospatial and semantic failures can remain visually plausible while still being decisively wrong for downstream use. To address this, we develop a novel human-aligned perceptual quality metric for generated RS imagery and evaluate it across several generation tasks. We first conduct a controlled perturbation study where we progressively increase the severity of common synthetic perturbations. Using the layer-wise Aligned Attention Score (AAS) responses in RemoteCLIP, we find that different RS artifacts are best captured at different depths. Based on these findings, we introduce Remote Sensing Perceptual Distance (RSPD), a pairwise metric that averages seven independently calibrated layer distances without tuning on human-preference labels. To evaluate our metric on realistic RS generation tasks, we construct a blind, reference-conditioned two-alternative forced-choice (2AFC) benchmark with five 300-triplet subsets spanning synthetic artifacts, airborne and spaceborne super-resolution, SAR-to-optical translation, and timeseries controls. We also construct a 300-triplet ordered misregistration benchmark with deterministic labels. Using the same RemoteCLIP checkpoint, RSPD substantially outperforms simple cosine distance with the final global embedding and is competitive with the strongest generic baselines across the realistic RS generation tasks.
  </details>

- **[Sketch, Don't Paint: Allocating Test-Time Compute in Generative Visual Reasoning](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21812)**  `arXiv:2611.21812`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sheng Lu, Guangpin Tao, Tong Lu, Ying Tai_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Multimodal models increasingly reason by synthesizing pixels, sketching maze paths within interleaved chains of thought or rolling out video solutions frame by frame. Each visual thought runs the full diffusion schedule inherited from photorealistic synthesis, so iterative denoising dominates inference cost. We study how to allocate test-time compute along two axes: when to render visual thoughts rather than thinking in text or answering directly, and how many denoising steps each thought requires. Controlled interventions that vary only how the thought is rendered uncover three regimes. Causal thoughts carry the plan and become legible early: an 8-step sketch nearly matches a 50-step render in accuracy, commits to the same plan as often as two independent full renders agree, and in video models mostly fails by stalling short of the goal, so the rollout abstains. Decorative thoughts can be omitted without loss, whereas harmful thoughts lower accuracy. Because the regime depends on both model and task, always drawing is suboptimal. Across 14 model-benchmark pairs of three interleaved reasoners, answering directly, without any thinking, wins 9 pairs, and choosing among direct, text-only, and visual tiers from ten labeled examples per pair raises mean accuracy from 60.7% (always drawing) to 65.1%. Where drawing pays, we propose sketch-and-vote, which samples 8-step sketches (a step count chosen without labels) from independent noises until one answer reaches three votes. With this one setting, sketch-and-vote beats full rendering with fewer denoising steps on an image reasoner and two video reasoners, by 8.6 points on VSP spatial planning and by 9.6 and 22.1 points on mazes.
  </details>

- **[Every Camera Grows Up: Camera-Height Regression in Interactive Video World Models](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21782)**  `arXiv:2611.21782`  `cs.CV`  
  _Sheng Lu, Guangpin Tao, Tong Lu, Ying Tai_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Interactive video world models serve as simulators for embodied agents: from a starting image and an action trajectory, they generate the views the agent would see. The starting image fixes how high the camera is, for example 0.15 m for a floor robot or 2.2 m for an overhead camera, and a faithful simulator must keep this height as the camera moves. We show that six recent open-weight models fail to do so: as motion replaces the starting view with generated content, they move the camera toward human eye level, a failure we call camera-height regression. Our benchmark, EyeHeight, pairs six rendered elevations (0.15 m to 2.2 m) and four calibrated real-world rig heights with scale-free multi-view height estimation. All six models lose their initial height during a $180^\circ$ turn-and-walk rollout: initialized at 0.15 m, only 0-20% of clips end within $1.3\times$ of the starting height as inferred viewpoints converge to human heights of 1.1 m to 1.5 m, whereas ground-truth renders remain within a few percent. In Matrix-Game models, homography-tracked floor motion stays consistent with constant elevation during rotation even as newly outpainted room geometry yields a higher reconstructed viewpoint. Regression grows with rotation angle, begins while the start frame remains in context, and largely reverses in SANA-WM when a $360^\circ$ turn restores the initial view. We trace this pull to human-centric video priors in which floor-level views are nearly absent: in SANA-WM, textual height cues and dense floor-disparity maps do not prevent it, whereas classifier-free text guidance during the first half of denoising strengthens it. Combining unguided sampling with height-balanced fine-tuning recovers more retention but costs 5 MUSIQ points, a loss fixed during those same early steps. Viewpoint drift alone shifts the paths predicted by visual navigation policies.
  </details>

- **[DuoFlow: Towards Synchronized Speech and Motion Generation with Fine-Grained Control](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21530)**  `arXiv:2611.21530`  `cs.CV`  
  _Jun Dong, Ronghui Li, Zhongyuan Hu, Yunqi Zhao, Lijie Guo, Ruizhang Xu, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  An embodied speaker's expressiveness depends not only on what is said, but also on how the voice sounds and how the body moves. However, existing joint speech-motion generation models are largely driven implicitly by transcript semantics or intermediate speech representations, offering little explicit control over motion hierarchy, body parts, or vocal style. To bridge this gap, we construct MotionSpeech, a structured benchmark for controllable joint speech-motion generation that includes transcripts, voice-style descriptions, and motion conditions at two hierarchical levels. Building on this benchmark, we propose DuoFlow, a dual-stream latent flow transformer that jointly denoises speech and motion under a shared noise level and a unified second-based temporal coordinate. Modality-specific residual connectors inject structured conditions into the corresponding streams, layer-wise bidirectional cross-modal attention coordinates their generation, and a monotonic phoneme pathway combines normalized progress anchors with CTC supervision to strengthen speech temporal modeling. DuoFlow provides a unified interface for fine-grained motion and vocal-style control and demonstrates the ability to synthesize high-quality, synchronized, and instruction-consistent speech-motion sequences. Project page,this https URL.
  </details>

- **[IGRecon4D: Dynamic-Object-Aware Feed-Forward Reconstruction of Driving Scenes](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21126)**  `arXiv:2611.21126`  `cs.CV`  
  _Yifei Tong, Ziyang Huang, Yizhao Han, Xiaoxiao Long, Hao Zhu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Feed-forward 4D driving-scene reconstruction enables efficient modeling of dynamic autonomous-driving scenes, but current methods suffer from two complementary failure modes: semantic leakage of traffic participants into the static representation and geometric drift of Gaussian locations across viewpoints. We propose \textbf{IGRecon4D}, a feed-forward dynamic scene reconstruction framework that addresses these failures with training-time instance-prior distillation and LiDAR-anchored Gaussian positioning. Specifically, an instance-level semantic branch provides a potentially dynamic participant prior, which is distilled into dynamic-mask learning through the Instance-Dynamic Consistency Constraint to encourage high-recall localization of traffic-participant regions prone to dynamic-static leakage. In parallel, sparse LiDAR measurements guide dense depth estimation, and the resulting depths are back-projected to anchor Gaussian positions for improved multi-view geometric consistency. Experiments on the Waymo Open Dataset and zero-shot evaluation on nuScenes demonstrate improved full-image reconstruction on the main benchmark and stronger traffic-participant-region reconstruction under cross-dataset evaluation.
  </details>

- **[ScoutCache: Efficient Few-Step Autoregressive Video Diffusion via Conditional Readout and Selective Caching](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.21038)**  `arXiv:2611.21038`  `cs.CV`  
  _Hao Tan, Jinghui Qin, Xuming Hu_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Few-step autoregressive video diffusion enables efficient long video generation, but inference still incurs substantial computational costs from repeated denoising and KV cache operations. Existing acceleration methods reduce parts of this overhead, while historical KV reading and cache construction remain costly across video chunks. To address these challenges, we propose ScoutCache, a training-free framework that jointly optimizes denoising, historical KV reading, and cache construction. We observe that shallow feature changes provide useful information for estimating clean latent updates, while historical KV dependence varies across Transformer layers and attention heads but remains relatively stable across video chunks. Based on these observations, we develop Conditional Denoising Readout (CDR) to estimate intermediate denoising updates from shallow feature changes and known noise variations. We further introduce Output Risk Guided Historical KV Reading (ORHR) to allocate historical reading budgets based on attention output perturbations, and Content and Coverage Guided Sparse Cache Construction (CCSC) to reduce cache construction costs by selecting informative spatial tokens while maintaining spatial coverage. Extensive experiments on Self-Forcing and LongLive using VBench and MovieGen demonstrate that ScoutCache achieves up to 2.28$\times$ and 1.86$\times$ speedups, respectively, while consistently improving generation quality without modifying pretrained model parameters.
  </details>

- **[FAVR: Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment for Vision-Language-Action Policies](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20971)**  `arXiv:2611.20971`  `cs.CV`  
  _Qixuan Wu, Xiaole Yang, Yuguang Yang, Canyu Chen, Linlin Yang, Haixia Pan, et al._
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Pretrained video representations provide rich temporal information for robotic manipulation, yet effectively integrating them into existing vision-language-action (VLA) policies remains challenging. We present Flow-Aware Visual Residual Alignment (FAVR), a lightweight framework for flow-aware visual conditioning of frozen VLA policies. FAVR maintains a compact temporal visual state through linear Test-Time Training (TTT) and retrieves action-conditioned visual features throughout flow-based action generation. By exploiting the affine structure of the frozen action head, we explicitly characterize how injected visual features influence predicted action velocities and formulate flow-aware channel selection as a box-constrained optimization problem. We develop a FISTA-based solver for joint gate optimization and derive an efficient closed-form approximation through diagonalization and rescaling. With only 1.74-2.19M trainable parameters and 5,000 adaptation steps, FAVR improves pretrained VLA policies across benchmarks. On RoboTwin 2.0, FAVR improves LingBot-VLA from 87.12% to 90.04% under clean conditions and from 86.88% to 89.38% under randomized conditions. A controlled ablation on five RoboTwin tasks further shows that flow-aware gating increases average success from 72.0% with a gate shared across flow steps to 82.0%, demonstrating the benefit of adapting visual conditioning throughout action generation.
  </details>

- **[ReScene: Incremental Scene Chronology from Sparse Revisits](https://arxiv.org/abs/2611.20433)**  `arXiv:2611.20433`  `cs.CV`  
  _Wei Qin Chuah, Zeji Hui, Amirali Khodadadian Gostar, Alireza Bab-Hadiashar_
  <details open><summary>Abstract</summary>
  Scene chronology aims to reconstruct a scene across visits separated by hours or days, during which objects may change in position, presence or shape. Such reconstruction must retain every earlier state, yet each revisit often provides only a few images. Storing a complete model for each visit duplicates the unchanged scene, whereas updating a single shared model alters earlier states.
